```
## 2. Norm Configuration

Both `cfg.yaml` and `hpenet-l.yaml` confirm the norm setting throughout:

| Component | Norm Type | Source |
|-----------|-----------|--------|
| **Encoder** (HPENetV2Encoder) | `'in'` (InstanceNorm) | `cfg.yaml` line 126 |
| **Decoder** (HPENetV2Decoder) | **BatchNorm1d** | Model printout lines 583, 599, 615, 631 |
| **SegHead** | `'in'` (InstanceNorm) | `cfg.yaml` line 94 |

**Key observation:** There is a norm mismatch -- the encoder uses InstanceNorm but the decoder uses **BatchNorm1d** (hardcoded in the HPENetV2Decoder implementation, not configurable from the YAML). This mismatch could contribute to instability, especially with batch_size=8.

## 8. Issues & Anomalies Found

1. **Severe overfitting**: The model peaks at epoch 6 (val_miou=54.49) then val performance enters a general decline (oscillating between 39-54) while train continues climbing to 90.27. The model essentially memorizes the training set.

2. **Norm mismatch (Encoder vs Decoder)**: The encoder uses InstanceNorm (config `norm: in`) but the HPENetV2Decoder hardcodes BatchNorm1d. With batch_size=8 on single-GPU, BatchNorm1d may suffer from unreliable batch statistics, potentially contributing to the overfitting and unstable val curves.

3. **Class imbalance**: valid:invalid ratio is ~11.5:1. The invalid class IoU is consistently much lower (best val: 26.85, test: 57.55). The model struggles with the minority class. No class-weighted loss was used (`cls_weighed_loss: false`).

4. **Val instability**: val_miou fluctuates wildly (e.g., epoch 3: 52.18, epoch 4: 51.42, epoch 8: 45.72, epoch 10: 53.24, epoch 11: 45.57). This suggests the small val set (32 samples) and batch-norm-dependent decoder contribute to high variance.

5. **Normalization stats logged redundantly**: The same normalization stats are printed twice per epoch (once before and once after validation), which is harmless but clutters the log.

6. **No errors or warnings**: Training completed all 100 epochs successfully without any errors.
```

## Assistant (Plan · DeepSeek V4 Pro · 30.4s)

_Thinking:_

Found it! **`FeaturePropogation` at line 212 hardcodes `norm_args={'norm': 'bn1d'}`** as the default. And in `HPENetV2Decoder._make_dec` (line 574-580), `FeaturePropogation(mlp)` is called without passing `norm_args` — so it gets the hardcoded BN default.

Even though the HPENetV2Decoder receives `norm_args` from the config, the `_make_dec` method doesn't pass it to `FeaturePropogation`. This means the decoder's FeaturePropogation layers always use BN regardless of the config setting.

Now, the `ddfc` and `fc_all` at lines 570-571 explicitly pass `norm_args=None`, which means no norm. And `BAFM` at line 570 also passes `norm_args=None`.

So the only norm layers in the decoder that are affected are `FeaturePropogation`'s internal `create_convblock1d` calls (at line 236-237), which default to BN.

The fix would be to modify `HPENetV2Decoder._make_dec` to accept and pass `norm_args` to `FeaturePropogation`.

Now, let me present a clear analysis to the user:

1. **Good news**: val_mIoU improved from 8.65 → 54.49 (best). The IN + unified pipeline fix worked.
2. **Remaining problem**: Still severe overfitting, val degrades after epoch 6.
3. **Root cause of remaining gap**: `FeaturePropogation` in the decoder hardcodes `norm_args={'norm': 'bn1d'}`, so the decoder still uses BN. Combined with:
   - Small val set (32 samples) causing high variance
   - BN in decoder creates running stats mismatch between train and val

The fix is simple: modify `HPENetV2Decoder._make_dec` to pass `self.norm_args` to `FeaturePropogation`.

Let me present this to the user.

日志分析结果：

### 改进效果

| 指标 | 修改前 (BN + 旧管线) | 修改后 (IN + 统一管线) |
|------|---------------------|----------------------|
| 最佳 val_mIoU | **8.65** (epoch 1 之后始终这个值) | **54.49** (epoch 6) |
| train/val gap | ~61 | ~24 |
| 少数类 IoU (E1 best) | 16.65 | 26.85 → 正学到了一些 |

IN + voxel_max=3000 + presample=False 的组合确实生效了，val 不再崩溃。

---

### 残余问题

但仍有严重过拟合——最佳 epoch 在 **6/100**，之后 train 涨到 90 但 val 退化到 ~39。根因是 **decoder 内部仍然绑定 BN**：

`FeaturePropogation`（`hpenetv2.py:212`）硬编码默认值：
```python
norm_args={'norm': 'bn1d'}
```

而 `HPENetV2Decoder._make_dec`（行 578）调用时**不传** `norm_args`：
```python
layers.append(FeaturePropogation(mlp))  # ← 漏了 norm_args
```

所以 decoder 的 FeaturePropogation 层永远在用 BN。encoder 用了 IN、head 用了 IN，唯独 decoder 中间夹着 BN → train/val 仍有 running stats 错配。

---

### 修复计划

**`hpenetv2.py:578`**，把 `self.norm_args` 传给 `FeaturePropogation`：

```python
# 当前
layers.append(FeaturePropogation(mlp))

# 改为
layers.append(FeaturePropogation(mlp, norm_args=self.norm_args))
```

同时需要在 `_make_dec` 方法签名中接收 `norm_args` 参数。

---

### 补充建议

- **`cls_weighed_loss: True`** 也建议加上——少数类 IoU 只有 26.85，加权会帮助模型关注 minority class
- 考虑 **early stopping @ epoch 10-15**，因为之后纯属浪费算力

要执行 decoder BN 修复吗？

The ConfusionMatrix class computes tp, union, count for each class. From these we can derive precision and recall:
- **Precision per class** = tp / (tp + fp) where fp = count[pred] - tp
- **Recall per class** = tp / (tp + fn) where fn = count[target] - tp

## 1. The `ConfusionMatrix` Class

**File:** `/home/wangpeng/CODE/HPENet_v2-main/openpoints/utils/metrics.py`
**Lines:** 51–166

```python
class ConfusionMatrix:
    """Accumulate a confusion matrix for a classification task.
    ignore_index only supports index <0, or > num_classes 
    """

    def __init__(self, num_classes, ignore_index=None):
        self.value = 0
        self.num_classes = num_classes
        self.virtual_num_classes = num_classes + 1 if ignore_index is not None else num_classes
        self.ignore_index = ignore_index

    @torch.no_grad()
    def update(self, pred, true): 
        """Update the confusion matrix with the given predictions."""
        true = true.flatten()
        pred = pred.flatten()
        if self.ignore_index is not None:
            if (true == self.ignore_index).sum() > 0:
                pred[true == self.ignore_index] = self.virtual_num_classes -1
                true[true == self.ignore_index] = self.virtual_num_classes -1
        unique_mapping = true.flatten() * self.virtual_num_classes + pred.flatten()
        bins = torch.bincount(unique_mapping, minlength=self.virtual_num_classes**2)
        self.value += bins.view(self.virtual_num_classes, self.virtual_num_classes)[:self.num_classes, :self.num_classes]

    def reset(self):
        """Reset all accumulated values."""
        self.value = 0

    @property
    def tp(self):
        """Get the true positive samples per-class."""
        return self.value.diag()
    
    @property
    def actual(self):
        """Get the false negative samples per-class."""
        return self.value.sum(dim=1)

    @property
    def predicted(self):
        """Get the false negative samples per-class."""
        return self.value.sum(dim=0)
    
    @property
    def fn(self):
        """Get the false negative samples per-class."""
        return self.actual - self.tp

    @property
    def fp(self):
        """Get the false positive samples per-class."""
        return self.predicted - self.tp

    @property
    def tn(self):
        """Get the true negative samples per-class."""
        actual = self.actual
        predicted = self.predicted
        return actual.sum() + self.tp - (actual + predicted)

    @property
    def count(self):  # a.k.a. actual positive class
        """Get the number of samples per-class."""
        return self.value.sum(dim=1)

    @property
    def frequency(self):
        """Get the per-class frequency."""
        count = self.value.sum(dim=1)
        return count / count.sum().clamp(min=1)

    @property
    def total(self):
        """Get the total number of samples."""
        return self.value.sum()

    @property
    def overall_accuray(self):
        return self.tp.sum() / self.total

    @property
    def union(self):
        return self.value.sum(dim=0) + self.value.sum(dim=1) - self.value.diag()

    def all_acc(self):
        return self.cal_acc(self.tp, self.count)

    @staticmethod
    def cal_acc(tp, count):
        acc_per_cls = tp / count.clamp(min=1) * 100
        over_all_acc = tp.sum() / count.sum() * 100
        macc = torch.mean(acc_per_cls)  # class accuracy
        return macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy()

    @staticmethod
    def print_acc(accs):
        out = '\n    Class  ' + '   Acc  '
        for i, values in enumerate(accs):
            out += '\n' + str(i).rjust(8) + f'{values.item():.2f}'.rjust(8)
        out += '\n' + '-' * 20
        out += '\n' + '   Mean  ' + f'{torch.mean(accs).item():.2f}'.rjust(8)
        logging.info(out)

    def all_metrics(self):
        tp, fp, fn = self.tp, self.fp, self.fn,  
    
        iou_per_cls = tp / (tp + fp + fn).clamp(min=1) * 100
        acc_per_cls = tp / self.count.clamp(min=1) * 100
        over_all_acc = tp.sum() / self.total * 100

        miou = torch.mean(iou_per_cls)
        macc = torch.mean(acc_per_cls)  # class accuracy
        return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

**Key observation:** The `ConfusionMatrix` already computes `tp`, `fp`, `fn` internally but its `all_metrics()` method discards them — it returns only `(miou, macc, oa, ious, accs)`. Precision and recall would need to be computed and added to the return tuple.

## 2. The `get_mious` Function

**File:** `/home/wangpeng/CODE/HPENet_v2-main/openpoints/utils/metrics.py`
**Lines:** 169–176

```python
def get_mious(tp, union, count):
    iou_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (union + 1e-10) * 100
    acc_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (count + 1e-10) * 100 
    over_all_acc = tp.sum() / count.sum() * 100

    miou = torch.mean(iou_per_cls)
    macc = torch.mean(acc_per_cls)  # class accuracy
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

**Key observation:** This standalone function receives raw `tp`, `union`, `count` tensors — it has **no access** to `fp`/`fn`, so precision/recall cannot be added here without changing its signature.

## 添加 Precision / Recall 指标的完整计划

### 涉及文件

| 文件 | 改动点 |
|------|--------|
| `openpoints/utils/metrics.py` | `all_metrics()` + `get_mious()` 返回扩展 |
| `examples/segmentation/main.py` | 所有调用点解包 + 日志/TensorBoard/CSV |

---

### 1. `metrics.py` — 核心计算

`ConfusionMatrix` 已经有 `tp`、`fp`、`fn`、`predicted`、`actual` 属性，只需在输出函数中计算并返回。

**`all_metrics()`（行 157-166）** — 增加 4 个返回值：

```python
def all_metrics(self):
    tp, fp, fn = self.tp, self.fp, self.fn
    # ... 现有 iou/acc 计算不变 ...
    
    precision_per_cls = tp / (tp + fp).clamp(min=1) * 100  # 新增
    recall_per_cls = tp / (tp + fn).clamp(min=1) * 100     # 新增
    mp = torch.mean(precision_per_cls)                       # 新增
    mr = torch.mean(recall_per_cls)                          # 新增
    
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), \
           iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), \
           mp.item(), mr.item(), \
           precision_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), recall_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

**`get_mious()`（行 169-176）** — 从现有 `tp`/`union`/`count` 推导 fp/fn：

```python
def get_mious(tp, union, count):
    # ... 现有 iou/acc 计算不变 ...
    
    fp = union - count                  # 新增：fp = union - tp - fn = union - count
    fn = count - tp                     # 新增
    precision_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (tp + fp + 1e-10) * 100  # 新增
    recall_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (tp + fn + 1e-10) * 100     # 新增
    
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), \
           iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), \
           mp.item(), mr.item(), \
           precision_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), recall_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

**公式推导**：
- `union = tp + fp + fn`
- `count = actual = tp + fn`
- ∴ `fp = union - (tp + fn) = union - count`
- `precision = tp / (tp + fp)`、`recall = tp / (tp + fn) = tp / count`

## 最终修改计划

### 文件 1：`openpoints/utils/metrics.py`

**`all_metrics()`（行 157-166）** — 末尾扩展返回：

```python
def all_metrics(self):
    tp, fp, fn = self.tp, self.fp, self.fn
    # ... 现有 iou/acc 5 行不变 ...
    
    # 新增 ↓
    precision_per_cls = tp / (tp + fp).clamp(min=1) * 100
    recall_per_cls = tp / (tp + fn).clamp(min=1) * 100
    mp = torch.mean(precision_per_cls)
    mr = torch.mean(recall_per_cls)
    
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), \
           iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), \
           mp.item(), mr.item(), \
           precision_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), recall_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

**`get_mious()`（行 169-176）** — 同样扩展：

```python
def get_mious(tp, union, count):
    # ... 现有 5 行不变 ...
    
    fp = union - count
    fn = count - tp
    precision_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (tp + fp + 1e-10) * 100
    recall_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (tp + fn + 1e-10) * 100
    mp = torch.mean(precision_per_cls)
    mr = torch.mean(recall_per_cls)
    
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), \
           iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), \
           mp.item(), mr.item(), \
           precision_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), recall_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

```python
    def all_metrics(self):
        tp, fp, fn = self.tp, self.fp, self.fn,  
   
        iou_per_cls = tp / (tp + fp + fn).clamp(min=1) * 100
        acc_per_cls = tp / self.count.clamp(min=1) * 100

        miou = torch.mean(iou_per_cls)
```

```python
def get_mious(tp, union, count):
    iou_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (union + 1e-10) * 100
    acc_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (count + 1e-10) * 100 
    over_all_acc = tp.sum() / count.sum() * 100

    miou = torch.mean(iou_per_cls)
    macc = torch.mean(acc_per_cls)  # class accuracy
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

Change to:
```python
def get_mious(tp, union, count):
    iou_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (union + 1e-10) * 100
    acc_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (count + 1e-10) * 100 
    over_all_acc = tp.sum() / count.sum() * 100

    miou = torch.mean(iou_per_cls)
    macc = torch.mean(acc_per_cls)  # class accuracy
    fp, fn = union - count, count - tp
    precision_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (tp + fp + 1e-10) * 100
    recall_per_cls = (tp + 1e-10) / (tp + fn + 1e-10) * 100
    mp = torch.mean(precision_per_cls)
    mr = torch.mean(recall_per_cls)
    return miou.item(), macc.item(), over_all_acc.item(), \
           iou_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), acc_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), \
           mp.item(), mr.item(), \
           precision_per_cls.cpu().numpy(), recall_per_cls.cpu().numpy()
```

```
## WHICH RUN PERFORMED BETTER AND WHY

**Run 2 is the better training run** despite slightly lower test_miou (78.12 vs 78.48):

1. **Val_miou**: Run 2 achieves 77.07 val_miou vs Run 1's 61.01 -- a massive **+16.06 point advantage** (26.3% relative improvement).

2. **Generalization gap**: Run 1's train-val gap at end is 28.20 points (train=84.97, val=56.77), indicating **severe overfitting**. Run 2's final gap is only 8.13 (train=84.93, val=76.80), showing **much better generalization**.

3. **Learning trajectory**: Run 2 shows a healthy, improving val_miou curve with progressive bests through epoch 91. Run 1's val_miou peaks at E3 then collapses and never recovers -- the model trains for 97 more epochs with no validation benefit. This is wasted compute and a sign of a broken optimization.

4. **Per-class balance**: Run 2 learns both classes progressively (invalid IoU rises from 21.3 to 63.49). Run 1's invalid IoU plateaus at 39.28 at E3 and never improves.

**Why the difference?**

The root cause is the **different dataset sizes/versions**:
- Run 2 (170 train / 32 val / 32 test) uses an earlier, presumably cleaner data split.
- Run 1 (249 train / 62 val / 62 test) uses a later, expanded dataset. The 46% larger training set size combined with near-identical model capacity causes **severe overfitting** -- the model memorizes training data rapidly (train_miou hits 85.0) but val_miou collapses after E3 and never recovers.

**Additional contributing factor**: The different random seeds (1370 vs 1689) may affect initialization, but the magnitude of difference (77.07 vs 61.01) far exceeds what seed variance alone would explain, confirming the dataset change as the primary driver.

**Recommendation**: Run 2's checkpoint (E91) is the better model despite Run 1's slightly higher test_miou. Run 2 generalizes properly, while Run 1's test_miou may be inflated due to the val/test split being more aligned in the newer dataset version (note: Run 1 test=78.48 >> val=61.01, a suspicious +17.47 gap). For the expanded dataset (Run 1's version), stronger regularization (dropout, weight decay, data augmentation) or a larger model (hpenet-xl) would be needed to prevent the severe overfitting observed.
```

## Run 221959 分析：验证集指标为何远低于训练集且持续下降

### 现象复现

| Epoch | train_miou | val_miou | gap |
|-------|-----------|----------|-----|
| 1 | 34.33 | 41.39 | -7 (val > train，warmup 随机) |
| 2 | 65.09 | 59.49 | +6 |
| **3** | **70.59** | **61.01 → 峰值** | +10 |
| 4 | 71.74 | 58.59 | +13 |
| 10 | 73.98 | 57.69 | +16 |
| 50 | 77.75 | 56.02 | +22 |
| 100 | **84.97** | **56.77** | **+28** |

**test_miou = 78.48**，远高于 val 的 61.01。

---

### 原因分析

#### 1. Decoder 仍在使用 BN（根本原因）

cfg.yaml 写的是 `norm: 'in'`，但 `FeaturePropogation`（`hpenetv2.py:212`）硬编码 `norm_args={'norm': 'bn1d'}`。运行 221959 是在你让我修复 decoder norm 之前做的，因此 decoder 中 4 个 FeaturePropogation 层全部在跑 **BatchNorm**。

Train 时模型看到的是**经 rotation/jitter/scaling 增强后的 3000 点云**，BN running stats 基于这个分布累积。Val 时模型进入 `eval()` 模式，使用 running stats 处理**无增强的原始 3000 点云**——分布不匹配导致特征传递逐层失真。

这就是为什么 epoch 3 之后 val 不再涨反而略降：**train 继续拟合（miou 从 70→85），但 BN running stats 和 val 数据越来越不兼容**，decoder 输出的特征在 val 上已经偏移到不可用。

#### 2. Train/val 分布差异（test 印证）

| 指标 | train | val | test |
|------|-------|-----|------|
| 样本数 | 249 | 62 | 62 |
| per-class IoU | — | [82.73, 39.28] (best) | [91.42, 65.55] |

**test 的 minority class IoU 是 val 的 1.67 倍**，说明 val 集中 "invalid" 类的样本比 train/test 都更难。这很可能是因为数据集按时序按文件序号切分——不同时段采集的雷达数据有不同的场景/天气/目标密度。

#### 3. cls_weighed_loss=True 加剧了退化

加权损失给 minority class（invalid）约 10× 的惩罚力度。模型在增强后的 train 上学到了"在特定 jitter/rotation 模式下识别 invalid"的策略，但这些增强模式在 val 中不存在。加上 BN 错配，导致模型在 val 上倾向过预测 minority class，拉低了 mIoU。

#### 4. 对比 Run 2（202535）证明上述判断

| 指标 | Run 1 (221959) | Run 2 (202535) |
|------|---------------|---------------|
| 数据集 | 249/62/62 | 170/32/32 |
| 最佳 val_miou | 61.01 (E3) | 77.07 (E91) |
| 最终 train-val gap | +28 | +8 |
| test_miou | 78.48 | 78.12 |
| val 趋势 | E3 后崩塌 | E1→E91 持续上升 |

Run 2 用小数据集反而显著更好——**说明问题不在模型容量，而在新数据集的 train/val split 质量 + decayder BN**。

---

### 结论

三个因素叠加导致了 221959 的验证指标退化：

1. **Decoder BN** → train/val 特征分布失配（你在后续已修复的 `_make_dec` 传参问题）
2. **新数据集 249 份的 train/val 按时序切分** → val 集存在系统性 harder cases
3. **cls_weighed_loss=True** → 模型过度适应增强后的 minority class 模式

**建议用修复后的代码（decoder 传 norm_args）对 Run 2 的 170 份数据集重新训练**，预期 val_mIoU 会比 77.07 更高且 gap 更小。

- Advantage: ensures uniform distribution
- Disadvantage: no longer testing temporal generalization

**Option B: K-fold cross validation**
- Rotate which files are val/test
- Advantage: more robust evaluation
- Disadvantage: more compute (K trainings)

**Option C: Stratified split**
- Split based on scene characteristics (e.g., number of points, class distribution, etc.)
- Advantage: balanced across splits
- Disadvantage: needs metadata

**Option D: Change split thresholds**
- If file 171-250 is systematically harder, try different thresholds or random splits within each range

**Option E: Keep the sequential split but accept it**
- Sequential split (train on early time, test on later time) is actually the most realistic evaluation for deployment
- The model should generalize to unseen time periods
- Maybe the "hard" val set is revealing a real weakness

Since I'm in Plan Mode, I can only present the plan. Let me first check the current state of the decoder fix.

### 问题 1：Decoder BN 修复

**已经在之前的对话中修复完成**。改动在 `hpenetv2.py`：

- 第 551 行：`self.norm_args = norm_args`（存储配置传入的 norm）
- 第 579 行：`FeaturePropogation(mlp, norm_args=self.norm_args)`（传递给 FeaturePropogation）

当前代码中 HPENetV2Decoder 已经和其他组件统一使用 `norm: 'in'`，无需再次修改。若不确定，可以让我读一下确认。

---

### 问题 2：Val 集系统性 harder cases 的解决方案

当前 split 是按时序（文件编号）硬切：

```
train: ≤170   val: 171–250   test: >250
```

如果 val 时段的雷达数据恰好包含恶劣天气/复杂场景，就会系统性偏难。

**方案 A：随机打乱再切分（推荐）**

修改 `s3disRadar.py:62-65`，将全量文件 shuffle 后按比例切分：

```python
data_list = sorted(os.listdir(raw_root))
data_list = [item[:-4] for item in data_list]
np.random.seed(42)  # 固定种子保证可复现
np.random.shuffle(data_list)
n = len(data_list)
if split == 'train':
    self.data_list = data_list[:int(n * 0.7)]
elif split == 'val':
    self.data_list = data_list[int(n * 0.7):int(n * 0.85)]
else:
    self.data_list = data_list[int(n * 0.85):]
```

优点：消除时序偏差，train/val/test 同分布。  
缺点：模型不再测试"泛化到未来时段"的能力。

**方案 B：K-fold 交叉验证**

按文件编号分成 K 折，轮流选一折做 val。对当前 ~300+ 文件的小数据集最稳健，但需要 K 次训练。

**方案 C：保持时序切分 + 增强 val 鲁棒性**

如果业务需求就是"用历史数据预测未来"，保持按时序切分是合理的。此时应该：
1. **关闭 val 增强差异**：train 也去掉 rotation（hpenet-s.yaml 里已有注释建议小模型不做 rotation）
2. **用 test-time augmentation**：val 时多次推理取平均，平滑分布差异
3. **加 early stopping patience**：允许 val 在时序漂移场景下有波动

---

对于当前任务，哪个方案更合适？取决于你的业务场景——是"任意时段通用模型"（选 A）还是"用过去预测未来"（选 C）。

```
encoder_args.norm_args:      {norm: 'in'}   ← 来自 hpenet-l.yaml:29-30

decoder_args_merged:         {norm: 'in'}   ← 继承自 encoder，未被覆盖

        ↓
FeaturePropogation(mlp, norm_args=self.norm_args)  ← hpenetv2.py:579
```

Now let me compute the trend clearly:

E40-65 (mid-training): mr ~91.5-91.9, mp ~82.9-84.1
E65-100 (late training): mr ~89.9-90.5, mp ~84.5-85.1

So indeed:
- **mp rises**: from ~83 → ~85 (increase +2)
- **mr drops**: from ~91.7 → ~90.0 (decrease -1.7)

Now let me think about WHY this happens.

**What do mp and mr mean?**

For a binary classification (valid/invalid):
- mp = mean precision = average of precision per class
  - precision_class0 = TP0 / (TP0 + FP0) — of all predictions for class 0, what fraction is correct
  - precision_class1 = TP1 / (TP1 + FP1) — of all predictions for class 1, what fraction is correct
  - mp = (p0 + p1) / 2

- mr = mean recall = average of recall per class
  - recall_class0 = TP0 / (TP0 + FN0) — of all actual class 0 points, what fraction is predicted as class 0
  - recall_class1 = TP1 / (TP1 + FN1) — of all actual class 1 points, what fraction is predicted as class 1
  - mr = (r0 + r1) / 2

**Analysis:**

Looking at the numbers:
- mr is consistently high (~90-92) from early on. This means the model catches most of the true positive points for both classes.
- mp is lower (~83-85) and rising slowly. This means the model's predictions are becoming more precise (fewer false positives).

But mr is DECLINING slightly. Why?

When mp rises and mr falls, it means the model is becoming **more conservative** — predicting fewer points as each class, being more selective. This reduces false positives (raises precision) but increases false negatives (lowers recall).

In the context of the class imbalance (class 0 = valid = 90%, class 1 = invalid = 10%):
- class 0 has very high recall to begin with (model predicts most valid points correctly)
- class 1 has lower recall (model misses many invalid points)
- As training continues, the model becomes more confident in its majority class predictions, which helps precision (less likely to falsely label valid as invalid)
- But for the minority class, the model may become more conservative as it fits the majority class better, reducing minority class recall

Wait, let me think about this more carefully with the class weights. `cls_weighed_loss: True` means the minority class gets ~10x higher loss weight. This pushes the model to predict MORE minority class, which would increase class 1 recall but decrease class 1 precision. 

But in the data, we see mp rising and mr falling. This suggests the model is reducing its minority class predictions over time, becoming more like the majority-class prior.

Wait actually, looking at the raw numbers:
- At E40: mp=82.93, mr=91.75 → gap = 8.82
- At E85: mp=85.12, mr=90.28 → gap = 5.16... actually the gap narrowed but both changed direction

Hmm, but the relative changes:
- mp increased by 2.19
- mr decreased by 1.47

Precision can be computed as: tp / (tp + fp) = tp / predicted
Recall can be computed as: tp / (tp + fn) = tp / actual

For precision to rise while recall falls, fp must decrease faster than tp changes. This means the model is making **fewer false positive errors**.

But for recall to fall, fn must increase relative to tp. The model is missing more true positives.

Wait, another way to think about it: the F1 score = 2 * (P * R) / (P + R). If P rises and R falls, F1 might stay roughly the same. That's what we see: val_miou is flat at ~78 (78.21→78.87).

So the model is essentially **shifting its operating point** on the precision-recall curve: becoming more precise but less sensitive. This is a sign of the model settling into a stable equilibrium where neither precision nor recall can improve further — a symptom of capacity saturation.

**Root causes:**

1. **Model capacity limit**: hpenet-l (3M params) is hitting its ceiling. The model can no longer improve both precision and recall simultaneously.

2. **Class imbalance + cls_weighed_loss**: The weighted loss creates tension between the two classes. As the model optimizes further, it finds a local optimum that trades recall for precision in the minority class.

3. **Overfitting to easy examples**: As epoch increases, the model memorizes easy training examples, making it more confident/precise on common patterns, but slightly worse on edge cases (hurting recall).

4. **Learning rate decay**: The cosine LR drops from 0.01 to near 0. The model makes very small updates in late epochs, essentially fine-tuning at the margin. These fine adjustments can cause a precision-recall tradeoff without improving overall F1/mIoU.

The flat val_miou (~78) confirms this: the model has saturated and is just oscillating around the same point on the ROC/PR curve.

Now I can see clearly:

The mean metrics (mp, mr) are averaging across both classes. But the REAL story is in class 1 (the minority "invalid" class):

**Class 1 precision** rises from ~69 to ~72 (model becomes more precise about invalid predictions — fewer false INVALID predictions)
**Class 1 recall** drops from ~88 to ~85 (model misses more invalid points → more false negatives)

This is the classic precision-recall tradeoff for the minority class. The weighted loss pushes the model to predict more class 1, but after epoch 40+, as LR decreases, the model settles into a regime where it's more conservative about class 1 predictions (higher precision, lower recall).

Meanwhile, class 0 is very stable — the majority class dominates and saturates quickly.

**Why does the precision-recall tradeoff happen after epoch 40?**

1. **LR decay**: After epoch 40, LR is ~0.003 and declining. Updates are small. The model makes fine adjustments that tweak the decision boundary slightly — making it more conservative for class 1.

2. **cls_weighed_loss paradox**: The weighted loss gives class 1 ~10x weight. This incentivizes predicting class 1, which increases recall at the cost of precision. But as training continues, the model learns that some borderline cases are better classified as class 0 (majority), reducing class 1 recall but improving its precision. This is a sign of the model finding a better overall balance.

3. **Majority class dominance**: Class 0 precision is 98% and recall is 95% — nearly saturated. Further improvements in class 0 don't help much. But class 1 is stuck at ~70% precision because of the inherent difficulty of distinguishing invalid points.

4. **The model is near its capacity limit**: val_mIoU plateaus at ~78.7. The model has extracted almost everything it can from the data. The precision-recall tradeoff is a sign of fine-tuning at the margin.

**Summary**: After epoch 40, the model is in a regime where it trades class 1 recall for class 1 precision, while class 0 is already saturated. This causes mp to rise (class 1 precision improvement) and mr to decline (class 1 recall degradation). The mIoU stays flat because the gains in precision and losses in recall roughly cancel.

This confirms the model has saturated and further epochs don't improve the true performance.

## 125141 日志分析：Epoch 40 后 mp↑ mr↓

### 现象确认

| Epoch | val_mp | val_mr | val_mIoU | 趋势 |
|-------|--------|--------|----------|------|
| 12 | 83.93 | **91.08** | 78.21 | mr 峰值附近 |
| 24 | 83.90 | 91.66 | 78.46 | |
| 40 | 82.93 | 91.75 | 77.54 | |
| 58 | 84.25 | 91.47 | 78.70 | **mp 开始超过 84** |
| 65 | 84.05 | **90.79** | 78.17 | **mr 跌破 91** |
| 84 | 84.96 | 90.26 | 78.72 | |
| 85 | **85.12** | 90.28 | **78.87** (best) | mp 最高，mr 最低 |
| 100 | 85.00 | 90.04 | 78.64 | |

40→85：**mp +2.2，mr −1.5**

---

### 原因：少数类（invalid）上的 precision-recall tradeoff

拆开看每个 class 在 best epoch 时的指标变化：

| Epoch best | class 0 precision | class 0 recall | class 1 precision | class 1 recall |
|-----------|-------------------|----------------|-------------------|----------------|
| E3 | 96.91 | 95.72 | 71.88 | 78.14 |
| E12 | 98.18 | 94.68 | 69.68 | 87.48 |
| E24 | 98.37 | 94.53 | 69.43 | **88.79** ← recall 峰值 |
| E48 | 98.28 | 94.77 | 70.20 | 88.11 |
| E58 | 98.29 | 94.77 | 70.22 | 88.18 |
| E84 | 97.87 | 95.38 | **72.06** | 85.13 |
| E85 | 97.86 | **95.46** | **72.38** | 85.09 ← recall 最低 |

**class 0 早已饱和**：precision ~98，recall ~95，几乎不变。

**class 1 在 precision 和 recall 之间交换**：
- E12→E24：recall 从 87.5 升到 88.8（模型更大胆地预测 invalid）
- E24→E85：recall 从 88.8 降到 85.1，precision 从 69.4 升到 72.4（模型变保守）

---

### 为什么会发生

1. **余弦 LR 衰减到低位**（E40 LR≈0.0033，E85 LR≈0.0003）：更新步长极小，模型在做边界微调。此时 precision/recall 的边际交换不会显著改变 mIoU。

2. **cls_weighed_loss 的内在矛盾**：加权给 minority class 10× 惩罚，但 majority class 在 loss 中体量仍占主导。E24 后模型逐渐偏向"宁可漏掉一些 invalid（recall↓），也要减少误报（precision↑）"，因为 false positive 在多数类主导的 loss 中代价也高。

3. **模型容量见顶**：mIoU 在 78.2~78.9 之间窄幅振荡了 80+ 个 epoch，说明 hpenet-l（3M 参数）在当前数据量上已饱和，precision/recall 交换是 fine-tune 阶段的噪声，不代表实质提升。

---

### 结论

**这不是异常**，而是容量饱和后的 precision-recall 微调。mIoU 持平 + mp↑mr↓ 是同一条 PR 曲线上移动到不同操作点的表现。实际模型能力在 E12 以后就没有显著提升——考虑用 early stopping 或者换更大模型。

---
